# Doc_06 — Full Lexicon Development

**World: Lutheran Wittenberg & Its Congregations** (Atlas VI.1, era 7, `lutheran-wittenberg-and-its-congregations`, file-code `witt`)

**Status: APPROVED TO PROCEED** (self-disposed per CO-022; no escalation category applies — see §12). Four independent adversarial rounds (Round 1: 5 substantial, 8 cosmetic; Round 2: 2 substantial, 7 cosmetic; Round 3: 2 substantial, 4 cosmetic; Round 4: **0 substantial**, 4 cosmetic — **CLEARED REVIEW**) were run. Every substantial finding from Round 2 onward was residue of the prior round's own repair narration — a fix that mis-described itself, an enumeration transcribed rather than re-derived, a bracket-only removal reapplied after already being ruled insufficient — never a defect in the lexicon content itself. Independent re-derivation from scratch at every round (the tier table, the 72-entry set, the Apology reading, the quotation base, the master index, the reciprocity graph, the Excluded-row guard) reproduced exactly all four times. Round 4's four cosmetic findings were fixed directly at Revision 4 (§12); no further review round is required for a cosmetic-only disposition per CO-022.

## 0. What this document is, and the disciplines it applies

**What it is.** The Framework's Step 6 output for this world: a final tier assigned to every one of Doc_03's 72 candidate terms by the Framework's own Part III criterion, reasoned against Doc_04's thirteen confirmed gravities and Doc_05's lenses rather than carried forward from Doc_03's discovery-stage estimate (§1); a [CT] ruling for every candidate, with the contest type stated wherever the tag stands (§2); the three world-specific Distortion Risk patterns named once so that no entry repeats them as boilerplate (§3); the Apology reading OG-4 and Doc_03 §11 item 1 asked for, with the thirteen "Luther-only" Author Gravity flags discharged one by one (§4); the entries themselves, in Doc_03's own nine thematic clusters, at three tiers of depth (§5); an in-document master index and a computed Related-Terms reciprocity check (§6–§7); a confidence review (§8); every prior-document instruction addressed to this document, with its disposition (§9); the loci read (§10); open items (§11); and the document log (§12). **What it is not:** it is not a re-test of any Doc_04 gravity or a reopening of any Doc_05 finding (Discipline 3); it is not the Deployment Lexicon (Discipline 1); it is not Doc_07 (no integrated-lens synthesis); and it is not the Representative's voice (Discipline 6 governs what "from inside" means here).

**Discipline 1 — deployment chunks are scoped out of this document, under the project's own standing process, cited by name.** The Construction Framework's Step 6 lists "Prepare deployment lexicon chunks" among this step's activities, and the Lexicon Framework Part VI specifies them; taken alone, that would put chunk authoring here. But `Build/reference/method/CiC_Record_Native_World_Build_Process_V1.5.md` — the canonical process this build runs under — supersedes that for a record-native build: it defines **Phase A (Doc_01→Doc_10)** and a separate **Phase B (record-store authoring)**, places the mechanical lexicon split at **B-2**, and states that chunk views are **B-8**, "**GENERATED from records**," not hand-authored at Doc_06 — in the document's own words, "this is where a record-native build departs from the six worlds' history." This document does not produce chunks — no `wittlex###_term-slug.md` files, no Retrieval Front-Matter, no master `Lexicon_Deployment_Index.md` spreadsheet — because Phase B is where they belong under this standing process, not because this build is departing from anything on its own authority. **A precedent claim this document was handed was checked directly and does not hold, and is corrected here rather than repeated:** the drafting brief stated that most sibling worlds, including the two closest precedents, produced Doc_06 "as a single monolithic construction document without separate chunk files at this stage." A direct listing of `World-Builds/` this pass shows otherwise: the Gallic build (`Gallic-Monastic-Ascetic-Christianity/`) produced all 81 of its chunks and its master index at Doc_06, across three passes, and reviewed them together; the Hieronymian build (`Hieronymian-Ascetic-Literary/`) produced chunk files for its 15 Tier 1 terms at Doc_06 and deferred its Tier 2/3 chunks "to a later deployment pass — disclosed here, not silently skipped" (hal_Doc_06 header); Alexandria (45), Imperial-Juridical (12), Desert (9), Donatism (7) and Syriac (9) all carry `Lexicon-Chunks/` directories; the Cappadocian build carries three chunk files at its root; the Post-Apostolic build carries its chunks as a single `Deployment_Lexicon_Chunks` document beside its Doc_06; and nine worlds carry no chunks at all — Anabaptist-Movements, Latin-Apologists, Latin-Pastoral-Congregational-Christianity, Lollardy, Reformed-Zurich-and-Geneva, Second-Century-Greek-Apologists, Society-of-Jesus, Tridentine-Church, plus this build (corrected from "eight," Round 1 finding C3) — exactly the worlds whose builds have not reached Doc_06. **So the accurate statement is: every sibling world that reached Doc_06 under the pre-record-native process produced deployment chunks there — and this build's own governing process, not this build's own choice, moves that work to Phase B.** What this document does instead, so that Phase B's B-8 generation is a lift and not a re-derivation: every entry is written in the Chunk Template's own section order; every entry's tags, Author Gravity note, [CT] contest type and Reported-Experience Status are stated in chunk-ready form; §6 carries an in-document index with per-tag columns, a CT check column and an Author-Gravity-Risk column, built from the entries themselves; §7 carries a Related-Terms reciprocity result computed from the entries, not asserted. Aliases, Retrieve-When and Do-Not-Retrieve-When are the one Template element left wholly to Phase B (B-2's split and B-8's generation), because they are retrieval-engineering fields, not construction findings.

**Discipline 2 — tier is re-proposed, not inherited (Lexicon Framework Part III, [117]: "Tier assignment should be revisited as development proceeds; a term's true ecological weight is not always apparent at the candidate stage").** Doc_03 itself said this: its §0 calls every tier figure "a provisional estimate only," and its §10.2 expected this document "to demote roughly half of them to Tier 2 satellites once Doc_04 has decided which clusters are gravities." Doc_03's split was 39 Tier 1 / 27 Tier 2 / 6 Tier 3 — 54% Tier 1, which the Framework's own words at [150] treat as a signal that tiering discipline has not yet been applied. §1 applies it: every one of the 72 candidates is re-tested against the criterion at [121], and every entry that moves tier carries a reason. **The result is 18 Tier 1 / 48 Tier 2 / 6 Tier 3** (§1.2). No number was targeted; §1.1 states the test and §1.4 shows the reasoning entry by entry.

**Discipline 3 — gravities and ecology are settled inputs, not subjects (Doc_04 §0; Doc_05 Discipline 8).** No Doc_04 classification is re-tested and no Doc_05 finding is reopened. Where an entry's tier turns on a Doc_04 divergence (G4's prescription/reception split; G10's inverse divergence; G13's bar) or on a Doc_05 finding (the hubs at §10B; the prayer fold at §2.6), the dependency is stated and the finding is applied as it stands. The one thing this document decides that neither prior document could — which *terms* organize the ecology — is this step's own mandate under the Framework.

**Discipline 4 — read, don't infer (Doc_01 §10; Doc_02 preamble; Doc_03 §0; Doc_04 §0; Doc_05 §0).** Every quotation this document makes in its own voice was located in the vendored file at the line given, and the whole document was then re-run against the ten files by script (§10). Nothing below draws on general knowledge of Luther, Lutheranism or the Reformation: where a true thing about this world could not be traced to a rowed locus or to a finding in Doc_01–Doc_05, it was cut or named as a gap. R94's Named-Comparandum bar is observed — no "Here I stand," no *pecca fortiter*, nothing from the 1525 or 1543 texts beyond Doc_02 §12.3–12.4's disclosure characterizations, nothing from the *Deutsche Messe* beyond R59's named absence. No Latin or German lemma is supplied from general knowledge (Doc_03 Discipline 2); the one original-language addition this pass is the one Doc_05 §11 item 2 found in the vendored text itself (*Bekoerunge*, LC 3632–3634, at entry 9.2).

**Discipline 5 — name the layer (Doc_02 §2; Doc_03 Discipline 2; Doc_04 Discipline 3; Doc_05 Discipline 3).** Editors' sidenotes and introductions (Jacobs, Steinhaeuser, Lambert, Schindel, Steimle, Schmauk, Neve; Bacon; Morley; Cole), Bente/Dau's bracketed German-text insertions in LC and Ap, Smith's modern idiom in SC, Bacon's composite hymn English, Bell's 1646 English in TT, and the OCR-degraded *Bondage* are named as layer wherever drawn on and never counted as a voice. Every hymn phrase is Bacon's English unless the German is quoted; the two Winkworth and Massie pieces Doc_05 §11 item 1 identified are attributed to those hands. Every headword is an English translator's; the original-language forms recorded are only those the vendored English itself shows.

**Discipline 6 — World Meaning is written from inside, at Level 2, under the prescription/reception line (Chunk Template; Constitution Articles 23 and 30; Doc_02 §14; Doc_04 Discipline 5).** Each entry's World Meaning is what a participant receives on request at Level 2: the term's function in the world's own ecology, in accessible language, with no analytical-distance markers ("scholars," "the sources indicate," "reconstruction," "historically," "Lutheran," "the Reformation") inside it — a check run over every World Meaning before this draft was closed. The world's own words are quoted with their loci, because a locus is not an analytical marker. Where a locus inside a World Meaning is carried from a prior document without being re-opened here, it carries the build's standard pointer ("per Doc_03," "per Doc_05 §1.2," "[R45]") — a citation of this build's own verification chain, not an analytical-distance marker, and every *quotation* so pointed was in any case re-verified at its line by the script run over the finished draft (§10). Phase B's chunk authoring should strip these pointers from the runtime World Meaning, as the Hieronymian build did for its own chunks, since a chunk cannot assume the construction record is in view; the Ecological Function and Key Sources sections, which serve Level 3, keep them. **The line Doc_02 §14 drew binds every World Meaning:** what the household or the parish was *to* do is stated as prescription, in the world's own imperative ("is to," "should," "as it is set down"); what the movement *claimed* it did is stated as its claim; nothing is stated as any household's or parish's fact, and G13 is never cited as evidence of what any congregation was like (Doc_04 §3 G13's bar). This document does not choose a Representative voice; where a first-person form appears in a World Meaning it is the world's own quoted "I" (the catechism's, the sermons', the confession's "we"), not this document's.

**Discipline 7 — living tradition (Constitution Articles 28–29; Lexicon Framework Part VIII).** This world corresponds to a living tradition. **Every entry below reconstructs its term's function within the bounded window 1517–1545, as the vendored texts attest it, and makes no claim about how the term is used, understood or weighted in Lutheran churches, confessions or communities today.** This applies to every headword still in living use — faith, justification, the Word, catechism, baptism, the Sacrament of the Altar, confession, calling, vocation, the two governments and the rest — and to the two [CT] entries most of all, where present-day usage is one of the named contest types. Rather than repeat this sentence sixty-six times, each Tier 1 and Tier 2 entry carries a one-line "Living tradition" field pointing here; the field is a distinction, not a disclaimer, and it is the entry's own Article 28 statement. Where a Distortion Risk section names a "modern hearing," it names a *reader's* likely assumption, never a living community's self-understanding. Living Tradition Status Confirmation (Article 29) is a freeze-eligibility gate outside this document's scope and is not claimed here.

**Discipline 8 — confidence inheritance (Lexicon Framework [43]; Constitution Article 17; Doc_02 §14).** Every claim about how a term *functioned* is calibrated in the five-level vocabulary exactly as Doc_02 §14 set it for this world: the doctrinal and confessional content, the founder's registers and the household-catechetical prescriptions are **Documented**; the movement's claimed parish practice is **Widely Accepted** as claim; every Table Talk saying is **Contested** as verbatim; everything about reception at the parish or household level is **Inferential/Thin**; and the reading of reception as such is **Contested** (the Strauss debate, Doc_02 §8 item 1 [R70–R72]). A term well-attested as *existing* is not thereby granted certainty about how it was *held*; the household entries (4.1, 4.2, 4.8) and every entry that touches reception carry Doc_04's G4 qualifier explicitly. The ten [AS] tags remain **Inferential/Thin** as comparative claims (Doc_03 §0; OG-3), unchanged, since no neighbour's lexicon exists yet.

**Discipline 9 — cross-world consistency (Lexicon Framework Part VII).** Every entry is grounded in this world's own evidence only. Nothing is imported from the Gallic or Hieronymian lexicons, whose Doc_06s were read for structure and for known error classes (Discipline 10) and not for content; where a [SC]-tagged term here has a namesake in another built world (grace, merit, confession, obedience, perfection, the devil, martyr, patience), no cross-reference is made and no equivalence of meaning is assumed. Apparent similarity is Differentiation Testing's finding to make later, not this document's assumption.

**Discipline 10 — known error classes from the sibling Doc_06 reviews, carried as a checklist.** From the Gallic Doc_06 Round 1 review and Round 2 spot-check: **S1** (Tier 3 entries omitting the Distortion Risk section the Framework requires at every tier, while carrying Key Sources the Template says to omit); **S3** (a pruning criterion attributed to Doc_04 in quotation marks that Doc_04 does not contain — this document quotes Doc_04 only where it quotes it, and states its own criterion as its own at §1.1); **S5/S6** (index columns reporting Excluded rows as sources, or reporting something other than author gravity under "Author-Gravity-Risk" — §6's columns are derived from each entry's own Key Sources and AG note); **S7** (a tier group mis-counted and one term left with no tier reasoning anywhere — §1.3 lists all 72, and §1.4 gives a reason for every mover); **C1** (phrases quoted from Doc_05 that Doc_05 does not contain verbatim); **C5** (a tag changed from Doc_03's without disclosure — every tag change here is listed at §1.5); **C9/R2** (a fix that inverted the finding it corrected). From the Hieronymian Doc_06 Rounds 1–2: **F1** (misdirected internal cross-reference pointers — every "see entry n.m" below was checked against the entry list); **F2** (a false reciprocity claim in the master index — §7's result is computed, and one-directional links are listed, not smoothed); **F3** (an overstated "carried verbatim" consistency claim — this document makes none, having no chunks); **F4** (Writing-From-Inside leakage: "surviving articulation," "by this world's own report," inside World Meaning). Each is named so a reviewer can hunt for it specifically (§12).

## 1. Tier reassignment — every candidate re-tested against the Framework's own criterion

### 1.1 The test applied

The Lexicon Framework, Part III, read directly this pass: Tier 1 is "Reserved for terms that organize substantial portions of the ecology, typically corresponding to Primary or Supporting Historical Gravities, or central conceptual clusters identified during Conceptual Ecology work. A world's Tier 1 list should be small relative to its total lexicon" ([121]); Tier 2 is where "the majority of a lexicon's terms should fall" — "terms that matter enough to include and that carry real distortion risk or technical weight, but that do not organize the ecology broadly enough to justify full Tier 1 treatment" ([134]); Tier 3 is for terms "that carry low distortion risk, limited technical weight, and minimal ecological centrality" ([142]), with the rule that "A Tier 3 entry that begins to require these [World Meaning, Ecological Function] should be reclassified to Tier 2 rather than expanded in place" ([144]). And the discipline: "A well-evidenced term that is ecologically peripheral remains Tier 2 or Tier 3. A poorly-evidenced term that is ecologically central remains Tier 1, with that evidential weakness disclosed within the entry" ([148]).

Applied to this world, the question put to each of the 72 candidates — stated here as this document's own operationalization of [121], not as a quotation of Doc_04 or Doc_05 — is:

1. **Which confirmed gravity does the term carry, and is the term the gravity's own organizing word or a satellite inside it?** Checked against Doc_04 §3's per-gravity Evidence and Dependency tests and §5's Interaction Matrix. Doc_04 §2.2's folds are read exactly as Doc_04 §10 item 6 instructs — "Doc_06 should not read any of the three folds as a demotion of the *terms*" — so a folded cluster's head term is tested on its own organizing weight, not disqualified by the fold. A Tensional gravity (G8, G13) is reasoned about on its own terms: Tier 1 may attach to it where the term organizes a substantial, bounded portion of the ecology (the ruling the Gallic Doc_06 made for its own Tensional gravity, adopted here as an application of [121]'s organizing-weight criterion, not as that build's authority), and does not attach where the gravity generates programs rather than content (Doc_04's own words for G13).
2. **Does a Doc_05 lens actually organize around the term?** Checked against Doc_05's lenses and its §10B hubs, chain and tension points — not against Doc_05's lists of vocabulary. A term Doc_05 *names* in a list is not thereby a term Doc_05 *built on*.
3. **Did the reading done since Doc_03 change the term's weight?** Doc_04 read the Apology's Articles XV and XXVII in part; Doc_05 read the Large Catechism's Lord's Prayer entire, Sermons 3, 4, 6 and 7, and the two untitled hymnal pieces; this pass read the Apology's Articles IV (to the end of its "faith alone" argument), XII (to the two-parts argument's close), XIII, XV and XXVII entire, XXIV's sacrifice argument, and the Large Catechism's First Commandment remainder (§4, §10). Where new reading gave a term organizing weight Doc_03 could not see, it is promoted with the finding stated; where it did not, the term stays.
4. **Author Gravity and evidential thinness are disclosed, never used to demote.** A single-register term that organizes a whole lens stays Tier 1 with its narrowness in the entry ([148]); a well-attested term that organizes only inside another gravity's context goes to Tier 2 however rich its evidence.

Retrieval need ([RT]) and distortion risk ([DR]) are not tier criteria on their own — the Framework makes depth "proportional to a term's ecological centrality *and* distortion risk" ([35]), and a [DR][RT] term that is not an organizer receives full Tier 2 depth, "the depth compresses, the structure does not fragment" ([138]).

### 1.2 The result

**18 Tier 1 / 48 Tier 2 / 6 Tier 3** — against Doc_03's 39 / 27 / 6. Twenty-two of Doc_03's Tier 1 estimates go to Tier 2; one Tier 2 estimate (4.9) is promoted to Tier 1; two Tier 3 estimates (8.6, 8.8) are promoted to Tier 2; two Tier 2 estimates (4.3, 4.5) go to Tier 3. 18/72 = 25%. The count was not targeted; it is what §1.1 yields, and §1.4 shows the work. Every one of Doc_04's five Primary and six Supporting gravities has at least one Tier 1 term (§1.5); the two Tensional gravities split — G8 has one (7.5), G13 has none, and §1.6 states why the second is a finding rather than a gap to fill.

### 1.4 The reasoning for every entry that moves

**Demoted from Doc_03's Tier 1 to Tier 2 (22).** The pattern behind nineteen of the twenty-two is the one Doc_03 §10.2 predicted of itself: it "flagged every term that *organizes* a cluster rather than only the cluster heads." Under [121] a satellite that organizes inside its head's context is Tier 2, whatever its evidence.

- **1.1 indulgence.** Doc_04 §2.2 tested the 1517 cluster and found that "no vendored text shows any Wittenberg-era formation activity — anything taught, prayed, sung, or examined — that is indulgence-shaped." It is the world's *occasion* [F-gen], remembered in 1529 "when the Pope with his letters and bulls dispensed indulgences" (LC 3805–3807) as a defunct practice. Doc_04 §2.2 said "Logged for Doc_06 as a Tier 1 entry regardless — a lexicon tier is not a gravity classification"; this document reads that as permission, not instruction, and applies [121]: nothing in the ecology past 1520 depends on the term. Tier 2, [DR][TC][RT] retained; the Apology's own indulgence material (§4) enriches the entry.
- **1.6 the keys.** Its two organizing claims are already other gravities' evidence: "the keys were not given to Peter alone, but to the whole community" is G2's authority sense (Doc_04 §3 G2 quotes the adjacent locus), and the keys as the ministry of absolution is G3/G5's "many absolutions." No Doc_05 lens organizes around the keys. Tier 2 at full depth; Ap XII (read this pass) gives it a second confessional voice.
- **2.3 good works.** "There are no good works except those which God has commanded" (v1 6817–6818) and "when they work at their trade, walk, stand, eat, drink, sleep" (v1 6863–6865) are, respectively, G1's negative (refusal of merit) and G7's content (the estate as the site of good works, Doc_05 §5). Both heads carry them. The [DR] ("works" heard as charity or ritual) is real and receives full Tier 2 depth.
- **2.5 grace.** The world's word for what G1 gives and G5 receives; "I cannot doubt I have a gracious God" (v2 15723–15724) is quoted at 2.8, and "not because I've earned it or deserved it" (SC 182–183) at 2.1. No lens organizes around "grace" as distinct from faith. Tier 2.
- **2.10 sin / original sin.** AC II's definition is Documented and is the presupposition of the whole justification cluster, but a presupposition stated once, in one article, in one voice as read, is not an organizer; Doc_05 §9A item 12 lists it as a pattern precisely as "the presupposition of pattern 1." Doc_03's own coverage note asked this document to extend the entry from the catechisms and Ap II before treating it as saturated; Ap II remains unread (§10), and the entry says so. Tier 2.
- **3.2 Gospel.** The content-word of G1 ("the true treasure of the Church is the Most Holy Gospel," v1 1387–1388) and the second half of 2.6; 3.1 carries the Word's power and authority. Tier 2 satellite of 3.1 and 2.6.
- **3.3 Scripture against Fathers, Councils and pope.** Doc_04 treats G2 as one gravity with two senses, the restraint/agent sense and the authority sense, and this document gives G2 one head, 3.1, whose World Meaning develops both. 3.3 retrieves for the sharp-form slogan ("under the bench") and for the two-pitch register difference Doc_04 §3 G2 and Doc_05 Discipline 4 name (Luther's sharp form; the confession's additive "Scriptures… the Church Catholic"). Tier 2, Luther-only for the sharp form, at full depth.
- **3.4 doctrines of men.** The object G2 refuses and G8's "free" things made "must": Doc_04 §2.2 folded "the refused practices" as objects of G2 and G8, "not forces." Doc_05 §7.1 gives it one boundary line ("The Word / doctrines of men") whose test — conscience-binding, AC 400–404 — belongs to G5. Tier 2, carrying the [TC] test as its distinctive edge; Ap XV, read this pass, doubles its confessional evidence.
- **4.4 *to have a god is to trust*.** The remainder of the First Commandment exposition (LC 521–680) was read this pass (§10): the definition organizes the whole exposition, as Doc_03 conjectured, ending "where the heart is rightly disposed toward God and this commandment is observed, all the others follow" (LC 679–680). But it organizes the *exposition*, and beyond it the definition is 2.1's faith-as-trust stated from the commandment's side. No Doc_05 lens is built on it; §2.4 and §6C do not name it. Tier 2, [AS] retained at Inferential/Thin with OG-3's seedbed caveat, at full depth.
- **5.2 baptism — the closest call among the demotions.** For Tier 1: it is the sacrament every participant of this world has; G7's ground ("Through baptism all of us are consecrated to the priesthood," v2 2176–2177); the daily drowning of the old Adam (SC 398–400); a baptism-sense of "faith clings to the water" that is G3's whole logic (LC 3921–3922); and the fence against the "new spirits" (LC 3799–3842). For Tier 2, which prevails: G3 has three Tier 1 terms already (2.7 promise, 5.1 sacrament, 5.3 the Supper), and every one of baptism's organizing claims is stated in one of them or in 6.2; no Doc_05 lens is built on baptism as distinct from the sacrament in general (Doc_05 §3 "no order of baptism" is a coverage finding); and the Supper, not baptism, is where the 1522 crisis, the examination gate, the G10 boundary and the only documented formation event sit. Tier 2 at the richest Tier 2 depth, with the *Taufe*/*tief* lemma.
- **5.4 "for you."** Doc_03 itself: "a satellite of 5.3 Doc_06 may fold in." Folded as a Tier 2 satellite rather than merged, because it retrieves for a distinct question (what makes the words *mine*) and carries Doc_05 §6C mode 6 in its own right.
- **5.6 the mass.** The Supper's public form and the [PV] arc (1520 abolition-logic, 1522 pace, 1530 "retained"), which Doc_05 §3 treats as real change. 5.3 carries the sacrament; 7.5 carries the pace; this entry carries the arc and the sacrifice question (Ap XXIV, read this pass at 8567–8806). Tier 2 at full depth, [PV] retained.
- **5.10 confession / absolution.** Doc_04 §2.2: "The place where G3 and G5 meet — 'many absolutions… to strengthen our timid consciences' (v2 15694–15695) — not a third force. Folded into both." Doc_05 §2.1 gives it a formative relationship (confessor → penitent), which is Tier 2 weight. The [DR] — that this world abolished confession — is among the sharpest in the lexicon and gets full depth. Tier 2.
- **6.1, 6.3, 6.4 — the G7 triad, one head.** G7 is one Supporting gravity with three words (Doc_04 §3 G7's title). The claim from which the other two follow is stated once: "all Christians are truly of the 'spiritual estate,' and there is among them no difference at all but that of office" (v2 2159–2164) — that is the priesthood argument (6.2) drawing its own conclusion. 6.1 estate (the refused pairing and its reuse for marriage and parenthood) and 6.3 office (the criterion and the pastor's office) are its two consequences; 6.4 calling is the confessional wording of the same thing plus the fence "regularly called." **6.4 is the closest call in the cluster:** for Tier 1, Doc_05 §5's whole Ministry lens is "redefined as vocation," Ap XXVII's "perfection with us is that every one with true faith should obey his own calling" (Ap 10019–10020, read this pass) is the confessional climax of the argument, and the fence is where G7 was reshaped under [F-rad]; for Tier 2, which prevails, the word is Melanchthon's (Doc_03 §10.1), Luther's texts say "office" and "estate," and Doc_04 carries the three as aliases of one gravity. One head; three satellites at full depth, each stating its alias relation.
- **6.6 Christendom.** Doc_03 §10.2 item 7 proposed one entry with 5.8. This document keeps two — they retrieve for different questions (what the sacrament signifies and what the Creed's article names, versus who is inside the whole body) — and makes 5.8 the head, since Doc_04 §2.2's fold names the body "that G2 constitutes… and that G3 signifies," which is 5.8's evidence. 6.6 carries the inclusive edge (Muscovites, Greeks, Bohemians "perhaps better Christians than we are," v1 12817–12819) at Tier 2; its one Apology hit is now read (§4).
- **6.7 pope / Antichrist / "the Church of Rome."** Doc_04 §2.2 folded the papacy as a gravity: "Recurs everywhere, but as the thing the ecology organizes *against*, not around… the closest call among the folds, because it is the world's most-named noun." Under [121] the same reasoning applies to the term: it names [F-pap] and is the object of G2's refusal and G12's frame; Doc_05 §4.2 gives it one of four authorities and §7.1 one boundary. It does not organize a lens. Tier 2 at full depth, [PV] retained and developed — the polemical "Antichrist" against the confessional "Church of Rome" is the one plurality a Representative must not flatten. **Stated plainly: this and 6.4 are the two demotions a reviewer is most likely to push back on**, and the case for Tier 1 in each is given above rather than suppressed.
- **7.2 the sword.** G6's instrument, developed inside 7.1's World Meaning ("the temporal sword… ordained of God"); Doc_05 §4.2 treats "the prince" under G6, not the sword as such. Tier 2.
- **8.1 vows.** G9's refusal; 8.3 is its affirmation and the household's site. Ap XXVII, read entire this pass, is the fullest confessional statement of the refusal and is placed here; the entry is the second-richest Tier 2 in §8. Tier 2.
- **9.1 comfort** and **9.3 assurance.** G5's affective content and its goal-word; both are quoted in 2.8's own World Meaning ("the greatest consolation," AC 546–551; "I cannot doubt I have a gracious God"). Doc_05 §8 uses both as vocabulary inside the conscience lens, not as lenses. Tier 2 each, at full depth; 9.3 carries the anti-"conditionally" edge.

**Promoted (3).**

- **4.9 hymn / German singing → Tier 1.** The one promotion into Tier 1, and the one place this document applies [148]'s rule in the promoting direction. Doc_03 held it at Tier 2 with "A Preface to All Good Hymn-Books" unread. Doc_05 read it (§11 item 1) and, across §3, found the hymn organizing worship's formation ("German hymns, which have been added to teach the people," AC 790–791), its memory (the old tunes kept and re-worded, Hy 1009–1019; the hymnal "with name attached," Hy 1084), its belonging (Vespers for the students, Hy 772–774) and its authority (the 1543 guard on "our book," Hy 1090–1094); §6B makes the hymn a memory institution; §7.1 makes "our book / not our book" a boundary. G11 — the Supporting gravity with the fullest non-founder attestation in the build (Doc_04 §3 G11: Walter, Karsthans, Spangenberg) — has no other candidate term; "German" and "vernacular" are not Doc_03 headwords. Doc_02 §3.1 names the hymn as one of the registers with the widest actual congregational reach. **The evidential weakness is disclosed in the entry rather than used to hold the tier down:** no hymn text beyond four openings has been read in this build (Doc_05 §13 item 8), the tunes are absent from the file (R27), and every English line is Bacon's composite rendering. That is exactly the case [148] describes.
- **8.6 fasting → Tier 2** and **8.8 images → Tier 2.** Both cross [144]'s line. Fasting now carries a Doc_05 §1.2 function ("The body's food is the 'must'/'free' distinction (G8) applied at the table") and a paired Distortion Risk (the assumption that this world abolished fasting, against "excellent disciplines for the body," SC 530–531); Ap XV, read this pass, adds the confession's own statement of what fasting is for (Ap 6950–6960). Images now has its own texts — the Third and Fourth Sermons, read at Doc_05 (§11 item 4): "we are free to have them or not" (v2 15062–15063), "images are neither here nor there" (v2 15204–15205) — and a paired Distortion Risk against the iconoclasm expectation; Ap 7610–7616 gives it a confessional voice for the first time. Neither can be a one-line entry.

**Demoted from Tier 2 to Tier 3 (2).**

- **4.3 "What does this mean?"** A form feature, as Doc_03 itself said ("probably a form-feature Doc_06 records under 4.1 rather than its own entry"); it carries no distortion risk and no ecological function distinct from 4.1's. Quick Meaning plus a one-line note; the form is described inside 4.1.
- **4.5 "fear and love God."** The formula that opens every Small Catechism explanation; its content is 4.4's trust and 2.1's faith, and its one confessional echo (AC 1233–1234) is 8.5's. Low distortion risk. Tier 3 with a one-line note.

**Confirmed at Tier 2 with a changed weight (not a changed tier).** 4.8 daily — Doc_05 §10B added "the daily rhythm (morning, table, evening) as the form in which all five Primaries were meant to be met" to the ecology's central set; the entry is written at the richest Tier 2 depth, and the case for Tier 1 was considered: it does not prevail because the rhythm's *content* is 4.1's, 9.5's and 5.2's, and the word itself organizes nothing beyond its own repetition. 9.5 prayer — Doc_05 §2.6 ran the six tests and found the fold holds: "prayer reinforcing eight gravities and organizing no content of its own." Tier 2 at full depth, with the whole Lord's Prayer evidence Doc_05 supplied. 1.3 contrition, 8.5 perfection, 5.6 the mass — each gains a confessional article read entire this pass (§4).

### 1.5 Gravity coverage of the Tier 1 set, and every tag that changed

**By gravity.** G1: 2.1 faith, 2.2 justification. G2: 3.1 the Word. G3: 2.7 promise/testament, 5.1 sacrament, 5.3 the Sacrament of the Altar. G4: 4.1 catechism, 4.2 household. G5: 2.8 conscience, 1.2 repentance. G6: 7.1 the two governments. G7: 6.2 "we are all priests." G8 (Tensional): 7.5 "must" and "free." G9: 8.3 marriage. G10: 5.3 — the same Tier 1 term as G3, carried there because 5.3's World Meaning gives the presence its own paragraph and states Doc_04 §6.3's inverse divergence (corrected this revision to name the entry directly rather than say "carried inside," Round 1 finding C6: 5.3 is the Tier 1 term for both G3 and G10, and the phrasing should say so plainly). G11: 4.9 hymn. G12: 4.7 the devil. G13 (Tensional): none (§1.6). Central conceptual clusters not themselves gravities: 2.6 Law and Gospel (Doc_04 §2.2's "shared hermeneutic grammar"); 5.8 congregation (the name of the body). Every Primary and every Supporting gravity has a Tier 1 term; no gravity has more than three.

**Tags.** Doc_03's Origin and Risk/Function tags are carried unchanged on every entry except these, each disclosed here (Gallic C5 is the error class): **8.6 fasting** gains [DR] (a paired Distortion Risk now exists and is the reason for the promotion); **8.8 images** gains [DR] (same); **4.9 hymn** gains [DR] (the promotion exposes a real modern hearing — hymnody as ornament — against "added to teach the people"). No [AS] tag is added or removed (OG-3 governs). No [PV] tag changes (5.6, 6.7, 7.4 keep theirs; 5.7 and 8.2 stay declined on Doc_03's own reasoning, re-read and agreed). The [CT] tags are ruled on at §2.

**Author Gravity notes** change on ten entries as a result of the Apology reading at §4 (1.1, 1.5, 4.6, 5.4, 6.2, 8.8, 9.2, 9.4, 9.5, 9.7 — corrected from "nine," Round 2 finding R2-C2 — with 4.4, 6.1 and 7.4 confirmed unchanged); each entry's AG line states its new value and the locus that moved it.

### 1.6 G13 has no lexicon term of its own — a finding, not a gap this document fills

Doc_04's G13, "hearers and repeaters of words," is "not a Doc_03 cluster at all but an attention pattern Doc_02 §3.1 and §11 documented" (Doc_04 §2.1). Its vocabulary in the vendored texts is the founder's own: "God does not want hearers and repeaters of words, but doers and followers" (v2 14687–14688); "the shameful vice and secret infection of security and satiety" (LC 86–87); "carnal liberty" (LC 79–80); "we are very cold and careless in praying" (TT 3147–3148, Bell's English, Contested as verbatim). Doc_05 §8 weights "coldness" and "security and satiety" as the named failure. **This document does not add a seventy-third candidate — on a substantive ground, not a procedural one.** The Framework's own Part IV licenses exactly this kind of late addition ("Entry development frequently reveals candidates missed during discovery… Revision is expected"), and this document's earlier draft cited discovery-stage exclusivity against that licence — a claim Part IV itself contradicts and Round 1 caught (S3), so it is not repeated here. The real ground is G13 itself: Doc_04 §3 G13 states in terms that G13 "generates programs, not content" and bars it from ever being cited as evidence of any congregation's state, so the vocabulary has no ecological function a lexicon entry could describe without breaching that bar — a reason Part IV's licence does not override. What this document does instead: (i) the phrases above are carried as the world's own words inside 4.1 (the LC preface's reason for existing), 6.5 (the negligent pastor), 7.5 (the 1529 reversal), 9.5 (the household's coldness) and 5.11 (the Supper despised), with cross-references; (ii) §11 item 3 proposes "security and satiety / hearers and repeaters of words" as a candidate for a Doc_03 revision or for Phase B, at Tier 2, with the reasoning that G13 "generates programs, not content" (Doc_04 §3 G13) and is barred from use as evidence of any congregation's state — a Tier 2 shape, not Tier 1. The absence is recorded here so that a reviewer does not read the Tier 1 set as claiming to cover thirteen gravities when it covers twelve and one finding.

### 1.7 Considered and not done

- **Merging 5.8 and 6.6** (Doc_03 §10.2 item 7 proposed one entry): not merged — see 6.6 at §1.4. **Merging 5.4 into 5.3**: not merged — same reasoning. **Merging 6.1/6.3/6.4 into 6.2**: not merged; four entries at two tiers, with aliases stated, serve four different retrieval questions ("are laypeople priests?"; "what is an estate?"; "what is the difference between a pastor and me?"; "what is a calling?").
- **Adding entries for the Holy Ghost and the person of Christ** (Doc_03 §10.3; Doc_05 §13 item 14). AC Articles I and III were read this pass (§4). Not added, for the reason stated there: both articles state inherited Nicene and Chalcedonian content the world confesses "with common consent" and does not develop as its own formation vocabulary in anything read — the Spirit's own work in this world's texts is stated as "through the Word and Sacraments, as through instruments, the Holy Ghost is given" (AC 247–248), which is 3.1's content; Christ as "the Mediator, Propitiation, High Priest, and Intercessor" (AC 624–626, per Doc_03 3.6) is 3.6's. A future reading of Ap I–III or of the Creed's Second Article in the Large Catechism (LC 2683–2755, unread by any pass) could change this; logged at §11.
- **Adding a G13 term**: not added — §1.6.
- **Re-testing any [AS] tag** against a neighbour: not possible — no neighbour's Doc_03 exists (OG-3, unchanged).

## 2. [CT] Contested-Tradition tagging — assigned, not defaulted, and the rowed-source problem stated

Constitution Article 26 §B (paras 544–545, read directly): the [CT] tag applies to "Terms and claims subject to live scholarly contest within the relevant subfield," is "a formational tool, not a disclaimer," and is "carried, as contested, into the Representative's live runtime vocabulary." The Lexicon Framework ([105]–[109]) requires each [CT] entry to state which of four contest types applies. Article 28 (paras 586–587) forbids fabricating "scholarly consensus" and requires that "Where scholarly consensus is absent, contested, or evolving, this must be stated explicitly rather than implied or papered over" — a rule that cuts both ways: neither inventing a contest nor presenting a contested term as settled.

Doc_03 applied [CT] to two terms, 2.2 justification and 7.1 the two governments, each with its contest type stated, and disclosed at 2.2 that "no secondary source is rowed in the Registry for [either] contest; the tag rests on the in-text tension and on this drafting pass's judgment, and Doc_06 owes a rowed source before the tag is carried into a Tier 1 entry" (Doc_03's own wording reads "this contest," generalized here with a bracket — Round 2 finding R2-C1; 7.1's own disclosure is worded again differently: "no secondary source is rowed for it, and Doc_06 owes one before carrying the tag"). OG-4 and Doc_03 §11 item 4 restate the obligation: "Doc_06 should either row a source for each… or remove the tag; neither should be carried into runtime vocabulary on this document's word alone."

**What this document can and cannot do about it.** It cannot row a source: the Source Registry is Approved to Proceed and append-only under its owner's living-document protocol, and every secondary row in it (R70–R82) was rowed for the reception debate, the biography, the 1543 treatise, the door-posting, 1525 and the apocalyptic frame — none for either of these contests. It has not used live research (§12). What it can do is (i) confirm from the vendored texts themselves that the *in-text* ground for each contest is Documented; (ii) state the contest type and the contest as specifically as the in-file evidence allows; (iii) rule on the tag's status; and (iv) propose, for the Registry owner, named acquisition leads from prior knowledge, flagged as such under the Registry's own existing convention (R72, R82: "builder-prior-knowledge… Flagged for second-opinion review"), so that the owner can row them or not.

**Ruling.** Both tags are **retained, marked provisional**, on the ground Article 26 §B actually names, stated in those words rather than as in-text tension (corrected this revision, Round 1 finding S5 — in-text tension is not itself the [CT] trigger; live scholarly contest within the relevant subfield is): **the trigger condition is asserted from builder prior knowledge and cannot be demonstrated from this build's evidence base.** Both contests are, in fact, genuinely live in the field — justification's "forensic" and "transformative" readings across the Finnish/New Perspective and older Lutheran-Reformed literatures; the two-kingdoms doctrine's contested relationship to Nazi-era *Zweireichelehre* appropriation and its modern two-governments/two-regiments restatements — but this document rows no source for either claim, and says so rather than papering over the gap. The Apology's own bracketed "to be justified… used in two ways" (Ap 1085–1087, read this pass) and the two referents "the two kingdoms" carries in this library (church/civil in *Secular Authority*; Christ/Satan in *Bondage*, per Doc_03 7.1) are Documented as *in-text* ambiguity — real, and correctly recorded — but that is a Distortion Risk and confidence-vocabulary matter, not by itself the live-scholarly-contest condition [CT] requires; the two are kept separate here rather than treated as interchangeable. Keeping the tags provisional, with the runtime consequence stated, honours OG-4's second clause exactly: **neither tag enters runtime vocabulary on this document's word.** Because this document produces no deployment chunks (§0 Discipline 1), nothing enters runtime vocabulary from it at all; Phase B, which does, is bound by §11 item 1: a [CT] tag reaches a chunk only once a Registry row exists for its contest, and if none exists by then the tag is removed at Phase B and the entry's Distortion Risk carries the in-text tension instead. This is the place an independent review is most likely to push back, and the alternative (remove both now, carry the tension as [DR] only) is stated as a live option, not suppressed.

### 2.1 — 2.2 justification: contest types "Meaning within its historical context" and "Relationship to present-day traditions"

*Meaning.* The vendored texts carry two wordings of what "to justify" does. The Confession: "This faith God imputes for righteousness in His sight" (AC 238–239, per Doc_03 2.2). The Apology, in one paragraph: "we maintain this, that properly and truly, by faith itself, we are for Christ's sake accounted righteous, or are acceptable to God. And because 'to be justified' means that out of unjust men just men are made, or born again, it means also that they are pronounced or accounted just. For Scripture speaks in both ways" (Ap 1080–1085); and again, "by faith alone we are justified, understanding justification as the making of a righteous man out of an unrighteous, or that he be regenerated" (Ap 1120–1122). Melanchthon holds both together on purpose; how the two relate — whether "accounted" governs "made," or the reverse, or neither — is a specialist question this library does not settle and this document does not settle from general knowledge. **Documented** that both wordings occur; **Contested** how they relate. *Present-day traditions.* The term is in active use in more than one living tradition, and the question of how each reads the 1530–31 wordings is precisely the kind of "relationship to present-day traditions that use the term differently" the fourth contest type names; this document characterizes no living tradition's reading (Discipline 7). *Proposed Registry leads, builder-prior-knowledge, not read, for the owner's decision:* a standard history of the doctrine's meaning (Alister E. McGrath, *Iustitia Dei: A History of the Christian Doctrine of Justification*, 3rd ed., Cambridge, 2005); the position that reads "made righteous" as Christ's real presence in faith (Tuomo Mannermaa, *Christ Present in Faith: Luther's View of Justification*, Fortress, 2005); and the joint statement in which present-day Lutheran and Catholic bodies addressed the same wordings (the *Joint Declaration on the Doctrine of Justification*, 1999). None is cited as a Key Source; each is a lead for a row.

### 2.2 — 7.1 the two governments: contest type "Historical scope"

The contest is whether the later systematic label — a "two kingdoms doctrine" as one named, organized teaching — applies as broadly to the founder's own usage as claimed. In this library the founder says "two classes," "two governments," and "the kingdom of God… the kingdom of the world" in *Secular Authority* (v3 12139–12141, per Doc_03 7.1; 12318–12323), "two kingdoms in the world mutually militating against each other" of Satan and Christ in *Bondage* (Co 14748–14750, OCR as found, per Doc_03), and never treats "the two kingdoms" as one fixed technical term spanning both senses; the running marginal "The Two Kingdoms" is the 1930 editor's (v3 12123–12127, OCR-broken across lines in the file; per Doc_03), and the confession says "the power of the Church and the power of the sword" (AC 1281–1285). **Documented** that the usages differ; **Contested** whether a single doctrine should be read across them. *Proposed Registry lead, builder-prior-knowledge, not read:* W. D. J. Cargill Thompson, "The 'Two Kingdoms' and the 'Two Regiments': Some Problems of Luther's *Zwei-Reiche-Lehre*," *Journal of Theological Studies* 20 (1969) — the article whose title is the contest itself, named as a lead for a row, not as a source. A second lead in English: William J. Wright, *Martin Luther's Understanding of God's Two Kingdoms* (Baker Academic, 2010). Neither is cited in the entry.

### 2.3 Considered for [CT] and not tagged, with reasons

- **5.3 the bodily presence / "in and under."** The controversy with the Reformed is the sharpest doctrinal contest this world was party to, but it is a contest *between traditions* about the sacrament, not a live *scholarly* contest about the term's meaning, scope or application; Article 26's bar is scholarly contest within the subfield. What this library shows is the doctrine stated three times without change (Doc_04 §3 G10) and the controversy absent (R56, R57 unvendored). Tagging would invent a contest the Registry does not record; the boundary is carried as Distortion Risk and as Doc_04 §6.3's inverse divergence.
- **4.1 catechism / 4.2 household — reception.** The Strauss–Scribner–Kittelson debate (R70–R72; Doc_02 §8 item 1) is a genuine, rowed scholarly contest, but it concerns whether the *program* was *received*, not what the term "catechism" or "household" meant or whether it applied to this world. It is carried as the **Contested** confidence tag on reception inside those entries, per Doc_04's G4 divergence, not as [CT] on the term.
- **6.7 "Antichrist."** The apocalyptic reading of the frame (Oberman, R82) is a rowed secondary position, but Doc_04 §3 G12 holds it "Dominant Modern Reconstruction at most and unread" and builds nothing on it; a contest about how to *read* the world's adversary frame is not a contest about the term's meaning in the texts, which is Documented.
- **1.2 repentance / penance.** The Jacobs footnote (v1 1522–1524) shows a translation ambiguity, not a scholarly contest; carried as Distortion Risk.
- **2.9 free will.** The Erasmus debate is this world's own argument, not a live scholarly contest about the term; the OCR problem is an evidence-quality flag (R34), not a contest.
- **The ten [AS] tags.** Their comparative claims are Inferential/Thin (OG-3), which is a confidence status, not a contest.

No other candidate carries [CT]. Two tags, both provisional, both with contest types stated: that is the whole [CT] set, and §6's index column records it.

## 3. Distortion Risk patterns — this world's own three, named once

Every Tier 1 and Tier 2 entry below states its own Modern Hearing / World Hearing pair in its own terms, and every Tier 3 entry carries at least a one-line pairing (Gallic Round 1 S1 is the error class). But three importations recur across most of the lexicon, and they are named here at the document level so that no entry repeats them as boilerplate. They are this world's own three, derived from Doc_05's ecology, not a generic list.

**(a) The systematic back-projection.** Because this world's vocabulary was later organized into systems, confessions and slogans, a modern reader arrives with the system and reads the texts through it: the Latin sola-formulas (which do not occur in any vendored file — Doc_03 §10.3), "the two kingdoms doctrine" as a named teaching (§2.2), "the priesthood of all believers" as a fixed phrase (Neve's and Jacobs's editorial phrasings, not the founder's — Doc_03 6.2), "the theology of the cross" (zero occurrences), "Law and Gospel" as a labelled method. The world's own words are plainer and earlier: "faith alone," "we are all priests," "two governments," "commands and promises," "Cross, cross." Entries 2.1, 2.6, 3.3, 6.2, 7.1 and 1.7 carry this pattern in their own terms. The living-tradition rule (Discipline 7) sits inside this pattern: the back-projection is often a projection *from* a present-day usage, and the entries name the gap without characterizing the present-day usage.

**(b) The "abolished" assumption.** A reader who knows only that this world broke with the papacy assumes it abolished what the papacy had: confession, the mass, fasting, images, the memory of the saints, Latin, the liturgical year, bishops. In this library the world *kept* most of these and re-founded them: "I will let no man take private confession away from me" (v2 15678–15679); "the Mass is retained among us, and celebrated with the highest reverence" (AC 787–788); fasting as "excellent disciplines for the body" (SC 530–531); images "neither here nor there" (v2 15204–15205); "the memory of saints may be set before us" (AC 620–621, per Doc_03 8.7); Latin "on account of those who are learning" (Ap 8512–8513); holy-days "observed without sin" (AC 391–394, per Doc_04 §3 G8). What it refused in each case is the *merit* or the *compulsion*, and the test is always the conscience (G5) and the "must"/"free" line (G8). Entries 5.10, 5.6, 8.6, 8.8, 8.7, 4.9, 3.4 and 7.5 carry this pattern.

**(c) The individualist hearing.** "Faith," "conscience," "liberty," "calling" and "the Word" are heard by a modern reader as private, inward, chosen and autonomous: faith as belief-that, conscience as a moral compass, liberty as freedom from constraint, calling as career choice, the Word as a text one reads alone. In this world every one of them is *given* and *bound*: faith is trust in a promise spoken from outside ("faith cometh by hearing"); the conscience is a court that is terrified and comforted, not a guide; liberty is "preached only to poor, humble, captive consciences" (v2 15915–15916) and bound by love to the weak; the calling is a station commanded; the Word is external and does its own work ("the Word must do this thing, and not we poor sinners," v2 14917–14918). Entries 2.1, 2.8, 7.5, 6.4, 3.1, 4.6 and 9.5 carry this.

A fourth, narrower pattern is worth one line: the **founder-only trap** (Constitution Article 26; Doc_04 §2.2's "Luther himself" candidate) — a reader who takes every term as Luther's personal opinion, and a Representative that speaks the terms as his, when the confessional voice (Melanchthon's "we"), the household register and the one non-founder trace each carry them differently. Every entry's Voices line and Author Gravity note exist to keep this from happening.

## 4. The Apology and Confession reading this pass, and the thirteen "Luther-only" flags discharged

OG-4 (owed to this document) and Doc_03 §11 item 1 asked for a full read of the Apology's Articles IV, XII, XIII, XV, XXIV and XXVII "before any Tier 1 lexicon entry in §2, §5, §6 or §8 is written," and Doc_04 §10 item 8 passed on the remaining thirteen "Luther-only, cross-register" flags for discharge. Doc_05 §13 item 14 asked this document to read AC Articles I and III. What was actually read this pass, stated exactly (full ranges at §10):

- **Ap Article IV (552–1457):** Doc_03 had read 552–771 and 1108–1125; this pass read 771–1457 — the whole of Parts 2–5 ("What Is Justifying Faith?", "That Faith in Christ Justifies", "That We Obtain Remission of Sins by Faith Alone in Christ") to the heading of Part 6. The article continues to 4183 (its "Of Love and the Fulfilling of the Law" section from 1457, and the reply to the adversaries' arguments); that remainder is **not read** and is declared at §11.
- **Ap Article XII (4896–5688):** Doc_03 had read 4896–4935; this pass read 4935–5234 — the eleven condemned dogmas and the two-parts argument (contrition and faith) to its Colossians 2 exposition. The article continues to 5688; **not read** past 5234.
- **Ap Article XIII (6415–6566):** read entire.
- **Ap Article XV (6613–6991):** read entire (Doc_04 had read 6888–6907).
- **Ap Article XXIV (8488–9560):** Doc_04 read 8486–8517, Doc_05 8486–8572; this pass read 8567–8806 — the *opus operatum* statement and the whole "What a Sacrifice Is" division to the Malachi passage. The remainder (8806–9560) **not read**.
- **Ap Article XXVII (9561–10194):** read entire (Doc_04 had read 10004–10017).
- **AC Articles I and III (166–191, 208–231):** read entire.
- **LC First Commandment remainder (521–680):** read entire, closing the gap Doc_03 §11 item 2 named for entry 4.4.

Ap Articles II, VII–VIII, IX–XI, XVI–XXIII and XXVIII, and the parts of IV, XII and XXIV named above, remain unread by this build; nothing below cites into them except by grep-located single lines named as such (§10). Saturation is not claimed for the Apology.

**The thirteen flags, one by one.** Doc_03 §10.1 listed seventeen "Luther-only, cross-register" flags; Doc_04 §2.3 discharged four (4.1 → "none" for the practice; 4.2 → stays for the household mechanism; 6.6 → unchanged, one Ap hit unread; 3.3 → unchanged, Melanchthon's softer form stands). Each remaining flag was tested by case-insensitive count across AC and Ap and then by reading the hit's context (§10 lists the loci). "Attested" below means the confessional voice uses the word in the sense the entry defines, at a read locus; it does not mean the Apology develops the term as the founder does. **Three counts move with the counting method** (substring vs. whole-word: 9.4 death 4/129 substring, 4/133 whole-word; 6.2 priesthood 0/12 substring, 0/13 whole-word; 6.1's estate pairing 10/6 substring, AC 0/Ap 4 whole-word) — flagged here rather than claimed exact to the unit (Round 1 finding C4); none changes the "Flag now" disposition in the table below, which turns on presence and register, not the precise count.

| Doc_03 entry | Counts (AC / Ap) | What was read | Flag now |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1.1 indulgence | 2 / 14 | Ap 5011–5019 ("they sell [without shame] indulgences which they interpret as remissions of satisfactions"); 6365–6378 ("Indulgences were formerly remission of these public observances… so indulgences were incorrectly understood to free souls from purgatory"); 10406–10409 ("the sellers of indulgences, who shamelessly preached intolerable lies") | **none** — both voices, three registers |
| 1.5 purgatory | 0 / 30 | Ap 5003–5011 (eternal punishments "commuted to the punishments of purgatory"); 6363–6365 ("that eternal punishments are not remitted except on account of the compensation rendered by certain traditions or by purgatory, Scripture does not teach") | **none** — the Apology denies it at length; both voices |
| 4.4 to have a god is to trust | 1 / 5 | Ap 1748–1749, a bracketed German-text sentence: "Trusting in our own fulfilment of the Law is sheer idolatry" — the same trust-and-idol logic, but not the definition of "a god" | **Luther-only, cross-register — confirmed**; the Ap echo is noted in the entry |
| 4.6 neighbor | 0 / 12 | Ap 2312–2318, 3067, 9908–9909 ("in love towards one's neighbor") | **none** for the word (the second table's sense); the 1522 "look upon our neighbor" measure of liberty stays Luther's |
| 5.4 "for you" | 1 / 5 | Ap 5159–5162 ("the words of the Lord's Supper clearly testify… This is My body, which is given for you"); 9182 | **none** as quotation of the institution; the "put yourself into this YOU" development stays Luther's — stated as a compound in the entry |
| 6.1 spiritual / temporal estate | 10 / 6 | Ap 9765–9767 (bracketed: "states of perfection, i.e., holier and higher states than the rest, such as marriage, rulership"); 6789 ("the title of a perfect and spiritual life") — the *Stand* vocabulary, never the spiritual/temporal pairing | **Luther-only, cross-register — confirmed** for the pairing; Melanchthon's "states" and "estates of life" noted |
| 6.2 we are all priests | 0 / 12 | Ap 6463–6477 (priesthood "not… sacrifice… but they are called to teach the Gospel and administer the Sacraments"); 8719–8721 ("as Peter teaches, 1. Ep. 2, 5: An holy priesthood, to offer up spiritual sacrifices" — of all the reconciled) | **none, weighted to Luther** — the universal priesthood is attested in the Apology's sacrifice argument (8712–8724), the formula is Luther's |
| 7.4 insurrection / common man | 0 / 0 ("sedition" 0 / 2) | Ap 10002–10006 (bracketed: "through such commendations Muentzer was deceived, and thereby many Anabaptists were led astray") — a 1531 retrospect on 1525, not the 1522 vocabulary | **Luther-only, cross-register, period-bound — confirmed**; the Ap sentence is recorded at the entry as the one confessional glance at [F-pop], and as a glance it does not lift R94's bar on 1525 |
| 8.8 images | 2 / 18 | Ap 7605–7616 ("From invocation the next step was to images; these also were worshiped… a statue of the blessed Virgin, which moved automatically by a trick"); 8946, 9001 (images among "trifles") | **none** — both voices |
| 9.2 temptation / trial | 0 / 6 ("trial" 0 / 3) | Ap 3737–3738 ("in the midst of good works, temptations, and dangers"); 5155 ("this faith is nourished in a manifold way in temptations"); 5550 (bracketed: "in agony of death and temptation") | **none** — both voices; no Ap gloss of a German word |
| 9.4 death | 4 / 129 | Ap 1439–1442 ("how will such persons sustain themselves in death"); 5550; 8577–8579 ("the terrors of sin and death must be overcome by faith") | **none** for death as the terror faith overcomes; **Luther-only** for the sleep/burial register (Hy 957–987) — stated as a compound |
| 9.5 prayer | 4 / 57 | Ap 6510–6515 ("why do we not add prayer, which most truly can be called a sacrament? For it has both God's command and very many promises"); 6925–6927 (sermons "of prayer, what its nature should be, and that we should be fully confident that it is efficacious"); 8712–8714 (prayer among the "sacrifices of praise"); AC 575–579 (per Doc_05 §2.6) | **none** — both voices, and the Apology's "command and promise" is the LC's own structure |
| 9.7 patience | 1 / 10 | Ap 1524, 2545, 4506 (patience among the fruits of the Spirit); 5303 | **none** for the word; the household "my whole life is nothing but patience" stays Luther's |

Also read while discharging: **6.6 Christendom**'s one Apology hit, Ap 5625–5627 — a bracketed German-text clause, "this cause, which is not only ours, but that of all Christendom" — which attests the word in the confessional voice once, in the inclusive sense the entry defines; the flag moves to **none, weighted to Luther**. **4.2 household** remains Luther-only for the mechanism, confirmed: "household" 0 / 0; "father of a family" at Ap 5739 (a hermeneutic remark about transferring "a precept from a father of a family to a pastor") and 10011 (a calling), neither the catechetical mechanism — exactly as Doc_04 §2.3 found.

**Two findings from the reading that bear on entries beyond the flags.** (i) Ap XII's eleven condemned dogmas (5027–5066) and its definition — "contrition is the true terror of conscience, which feels that God is angry with sin, and which grieves that it has sinned" (Ap 5078–5080) — are the confessional home of 1.2, 1.3, 1.4 and 1.6 together; the Apology says of the whole subject that "before the writings of Luther appeared, the doctrine of repentance was very much confused" (Ap 4936–4937), the one place in the confessional voice where the founder is named as the clarifier of a term. (ii) Ap XIII's remark that Absolution "is the Sacrament of Repentance" and that "Baptism, the Lord's Supper, and Absolution… are truly Sacraments" (Ap 6441–6443), with its reason — "these rites have God's command and the promise of grace" (6443–6444) — is the confessional form of 5.1's Word-and-element definition and is added there.

**AC Articles I and III — read, and no entry added.** Article I confesses "the decree of the Council of Nicaea concerning the Unity of the Divine Essence and concerning the Three Persons" (AC 168–170) and condemns named heresies; Article III confesses the two natures "inseparably enjoined in one Person, one Christ, true God and true man" (AC 212–213), the descent, resurrection, session, and the sending of "the Holy Ghost into their hearts, to rule, comfort, and quicken them, and to defend them against the devil and the power of sin" (AC 221–223). Both are inherited creedal content the world confesses "with common consent" (AC 168); neither is developed anywhere read as this world's own formation vocabulary, and the one clause that is — the Spirit sent to "comfort" and to "defend… against the devil" — is 9.1's and 4.7's content. Doc_05 §9A's note that the movement's Christology and pneumatology were absent from its patterns is answered: they are absent because they are confessed as inherited, not because the library lacks them. Standalone entries are not warranted on present reading; §1.7 and §11 record what would change this.

## 5. The entries

Organized in Doc_03's own nine thematic clusters, not alphabetically ([59]: "alphabetical ordering obscures the conceptual relationships the lexicon exists to reveal"). Each entry carries its Doc_03 number, its final tier, its tags (Doc_03's, with the three disclosed additions at §1.5), its Author Gravity note (Doc_03's, updated per §4), its Registry rows, and its Living-tradition field (§0 Discipline 7). Tier 1 entries carry the full seven sections; Tier 2 entries carry the same sections compressed; Tier 3 entries carry a Quick Meaning and a one-line Distortion Risk. Related Terms name entries by Doc_03 number and canonical short name; §7 computes their reciprocity. Confidence tags follow Doc_02 §14 (§0 Discipline 8): **[D]** Documented, **[WA]** Widely Accepted, **[DMR]** Dominant Modern Reconstruction, **[C]** Contested, **[I/T]** Inferential/Thin.

### 5.1 Pardon, penalty, and the treasure — the 1517 vocabulary

#### 1.1 indulgence / pardon / "letters of pardon" — Tier 2 (Doc_03: 1 ↓)

**Tags:** [SC] [DR] [TC] [RT]. **AG:** none — both voices (Ap 5011–5019, 6365–6378, 10406–10409; §4). **Registry:** R1, R2, R12, R25, R38. **Living tradition:** 1517–1545 function only (§0 Discipline 7).

**Quick Meaning.** A papal remission of the *penalty* owed for sin, sold in letters — the practice this world's first public argument attacked in 1517 and remembered by 1529 only as a thing the pope once did.

**World Meaning.** An indulgence, or pardon, is what the pope's preachers sold: a letter remitting the penalty a repentant sinner still owes, "so soon as the penny jingles into the money-box" (v1 1253–1254). The argument against it is not that penalty does not exist but that the pope's pardon reaches only "the penalties of sacramental satisfaction, and these are appointed by man" (v1 1278–1279), that "Every truly repentant Christian has a right to full remission of penalty and guilt, even without letters of pardon" (v1 1285–1286), and that "The assurance of salvation by letters of pardon is vain" (v1 1348–1349; the file prints this thesis's own number as "53."). Giving to the poor "does a better work than buying pardons" (v1 1312–1313). By 1520 the whole trade is named in capitals a "knavish trick" (v2 6499); by 1529 it is a memory — "when the Pope with his letters and bulls dispensed indulgences" (LC 3805–3807) — and the confessional voice explains what the word had once meant, "remission of these public observances, so that men should not be excessively burdened," and how it came to be "incorrectly understood to free souls from purgatory" (Ap 6365–6376). The word survives as the name of the occasion, not as anything taught, prayed or sung.

**Ecological Function.** The historical hinge [F-gen] each of G1, G2 and G5 reports as its own founding case (Doc_04 §2.2): the refusal of merit-by-purchase (2.1), of a claimed monopoly on the keys (1.6, 3.1), and of assurance by letter (9.3). Depends on 1.2 (the whole-life repentance the Theses set against it) and 1.4 (the "satisfaction" it relaxed).

**Distortion Risk.** *Modern Hearing:* "indulgence" as self-gratification, or as a purchase of forgiveness of *guilt*. *World Hearing:* a canonical remission of *penalty* only, which the Theses say cannot touch guilt, purgatory or assurance; the target is the transaction and the false assurance, not the idea of penalty.

**Key Texts.** v1 1230–1232 (Th. 21), 1253–1254 (Th. 27), 1285–1286 (Th. 36), 1348–1349; 1553–1554 (Jacobs's footnote: Luther "uses the terms 'pardon' and 'indulgence' interchangeably" — layer); LC 3805–3807; Ap 6365–6378.

**Key Sources.** [R2] the Ninety-Five Theses (Jacobs's English, with the file's own corrupted numbering — Doc_03 §0); [R38] Ap XII and VI on satisfactions, read this pass. Author Gravity: none; the Apology's account of what indulgences "formerly" were is the confessional voice's own history, not the founder's.

**Related Terms.** 1.2 repentance; 1.4 satisfaction; 1.5 purgatory; 1.6 the keys; 2.4 merit; 9.3 assurance; 3.2 Gospel; 6.7 pope/Antichrist.

#### 1.2 repentance / penance (*Poenitentiam agite*) — Tier 1 (Doc_03: 1)

**Tags:** [SC] [DR] [TC] [RT]. **AG:** none — both voices, every register from the first Thesis to the Apology. **Registry:** R2, R12, R25, R26, R37, R38. **Living tradition:** 1517–1545 function only (§0 Discipline 7).

**Quick Meaning.** The whole-life turning the first of the Ninety-Five Theses names — not the priest's sacrament of "penance" — which the confession later defines as two parts, terror of conscience and faith, and which baptism makes daily.

**World Meaning.** The first thing this world said in public was a sentence about this word: "Our Lord and Master Jesus Christ, when He said *Poenitentiam agite*, willed that the whole life of believers should be repentance" (v1 1154–1156), and the second was that "This word cannot be understood to mean sacramental penance, i. e., confession and satisfaction, which is administered by the priests" (v1 1158–1160). The translator's own note shows why the sentence had to be made: the Latin and German "may also be rendered, 'Do penance'; the Greek, on the other hand, can only mean 'Repent'" (v1 1522–1524). Everything the word does afterward follows from that opening. "Three parts have they made of penance,--contrition, confession, and satisfaction" (v2 8953–8954) — and each part is re-founded: contrition is found "only where there is a lively faith in the promises and the threats of God" (v2 8976–8977), confession becomes an occasion of God's promise (5.10), and satisfaction is Christ's (1.4).

By 1530 the word has two parts and only two: "repentance consists properly of these two parts: One is contrition, that is, terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin; the other is faith, which is born of the Gospel, or of absolution" (AC 344–349). The Apology says the same and says why: "In order, therefore, to deliver pious consciences from these labyrinths of the sophists, we have ascribed to repentance [or conversion] these two parts, namely, contrition and faith" (Ap 5068–5070) — and if anyone wishes to add a third, "fruits worthy of repentance," "we will not make any opposition" (Ap 5071–5074). It even says the term was dark before: "before the writings of Luther appeared, the doctrine of repentance was very much confused" (Ap 4936–4937). The two parts are the conscience's own sequence — terror, then comfort — which is why "the contrition of Judas or Saul is of no avail" and "the contrition of David or Peter avails because to it there is added faith" (Ap 5124–5128).

In the household the word is daily and bodily. Baptism means "the old Adam in us should be drowned by daily sorrow and repentance, and die with all sins and evil lusts, and, in turn, a new person daily come forth" (SC 398–400). Repentance is not an episode the priest administers once a year; it is the shape of a baptized life, and its second half is always faith.

**Ecological Function.** The confessional and catechetical *name* for G5's terror-then-comfort sequence (Doc_04 §3 G5; Doc_05 §8's "affective plot"), and the word on which the 1517 occasion [F-gen] turned. Anchors 1.3 (its first part), 5.10 (its sacramental form kept), 5.2 (its daily form), 1.4 and 1.1 (the third part refused); presupposes 2.1 (its second part is faith) and 2.6 (the Law that terrifies, the promise that comforts). A participant who understands this term understands why this world kept private confession and refused the sale of pardons in the same breath.

**Distortion Risk.** *Modern Hearing:* "penance" as a punishment or a penalty imposed by a priest; "repentance" as a feeling of regret, or a one-time conversion. *World Hearing:* one word, a whole-life turning with two parts — the conscience's terror under the Law and its comfort by faith in the promise — daily in baptism, sacramental in absolution, never a work that earns anything; and a word this world believed it had rescued from confusion.

**Key Texts.** v1 1154–1160 (Th. 1–2); v1 1522–1524 (Jacobs's fn. [1] — layer); v2 8953–8954, 8976–8977; AC 344–349; Ap 4936–4937, 5068–5074, 5078–5080, 5124–5128; SC 398–400.

**Key Sources.** [R2] the Theses; [R12] *Babylonian Captivity*, the sacrament of penance; [R37] AC XII; [R38] Ap XII, read to 5234 this pass; [R26] SC on baptism. Author Gravity: none — the confessional development is Melanchthon's own, the daily form Luther's, the founding sentence Luther's.

**Related Terms.** 1.1 indulgence; 1.3 contrition; 1.4 satisfaction; 1.6 the keys; 2.1 faith; 2.6 Law and Gospel; 2.8 conscience; 5.2 baptism; 5.10 confession/absolution; 9.3 assurance.

#### 1.3 contrition / attrition — Tier 2 (Doc_03: 2)

**Tags:** [SC] [TC] [DR]. **AG:** none. **Registry:** R2, R12, R37, R38. **Living tradition:** 1517–1545 function only.

**Quick Meaning.** Sorrow for sin — in this world "the true terror of conscience," found only where faith is, and never a thing the keys can manufacture out of a lesser "attrition."

**World Meaning.** "No one is sure that his own contrition is sincere" (v1 1264–1265): that is where this word starts, and the uncertainty is the point. The schools had "invented a so-called 'attrition,' which is converted into contrition by virtue of the power of the keys" (v2 8968–8971); this world holds instead that "A contrite heart is a precious thing, but it is found only where there is a lively faith in the promises and the threats of God" (v2 8976–8977). The confession defines it as "terrors smiting the conscience through the knowledge of sin" (AC 344–345), and the Apology adds that "contrition is the true terror of conscience, which feels that God is angry with sin, and which grieves that it has sinned" — a terror produced "when sins are censured by the Word of God" (Ap 5078–5082), and one that "flees from the dreadful wrath of God, because human nature, unless sustained by the Word of God, cannot endure it" (Ap 5102–5103). Contrition therefore never stands alone; it is the first half of repentance (1.2), and its worth is decided by whether faith is added to it.

**Ecological Function.** The first part of 1.2 and the Law's work in 2.6 as felt in 2.8; refuses the attrition-plus-keys mechanism (1.6). Doc_04 §3 G5 opens its Evidence on this uncertainty.

**Distortion Risk.** *Modern Hearing:* contrition as the adequate emotional condition for forgiveness — "if I'm sorry enough." *World Hearing:* a terror the Word itself produces, never sure of itself, worthless without faith, and never a merit.

**Key Texts.** Ap 5078–5082 (read this pass); v2 8968–8977; AC 344–345. **Key Sources.** [R38] Ap XII; [R12]. Author Gravity: none.

**Related Terms.** 1.2 repentance; 1.6 the keys; 2.8 conscience; 2.6 Law and Gospel.

#### 1.4 satisfaction — two referents — Tier 2 (Doc_03: 2)

**Tags:** [SC] [TC] [DR]. **AG:** cross-voice, one word, two referents (an alias problem, resolved here). **Registry:** R2, R37, R38. **Living tradition:** 1517–1545 function only.

**Quick Meaning.** One word for two opposite things: the penitent's own "satisfaction," the third part of the old penance which indulgences relaxed and this world refuses; and Christ's "satisfaction for our sins," which is the ground of everything.

**World Meaning.** (a) The satisfaction a sinner makes: "these 'graces of pardon' concern only the penalties of sacramental satisfaction, and these are appointed by man" (v1 1278–1279); "human traditions instituted to propitiate God, to merit grace, and to make satisfaction for sins, are opposed to the Gospel" (AC 400–404); "Aforetime satisfactions were immoderately extolled" (AC 916–917). The Apology's whole third act on repentance is this — satisfactions made of "pilgrimages, rosaries, or similar observances which do not have the command of God" (Ap 5009–5011), redeemed by indulgences, so that "the subject of satisfactions is infinite" (Ap 5020). (b) The satisfaction Christ made: Christ "who, by His death, has made satisfaction for our sins" (AC 237–238); "Christ's passion was an oblation and satisfaction" (AC 847). The same confession uses the same English word for the thing it refuses and the thing it rests on; the difference is *whose*. What a sinner cannot make, Christ has made; and "there has been only one propitiatory sacrifice in the world, namely, the death of Christ" (Ap 8672–8673).

**Ecological Function.** The refused third part of 1.2 and the object of 1.1; in its second sense the ground of 2.2 and 2.4 (merit is Christ's). The alias must be kept apart at runtime: a participant who hears "satisfaction" in sense (a) inside a sentence about sense (b) has lost the argument.

**Distortion Risk.** *Modern Hearing:* the two referents collapse — "satisfaction" as a single doctrine, or as contentment. *World Hearing:* two referents held apart by ownership: ours, refused; Christ's, sufficient.

**Key Texts.** AC 237–238 against AC 400–404; Ap 5003–5020; Ap 8672–8673. **Key Sources.** [R37] AC IV, XV, XXIV; [R38] Ap XII, XXIV. Author Gravity: none.

**Related Terms.** 1.1 indulgence; 1.2 repentance; 2.2 justification; 2.4 merit; 5.6 the mass.

#### 1.5 purgatory — Tier 2 (Doc_03: 2)

**Tags:** [SC] [RT] [DR]. **AG:** none — both voices (Ap 5003–5011, 6363–6365; §4; Doc_03 had "Luther-only, cross-register"). **Registry:** R2, R28, R38. **Living tradition:** 1517–1545 function only.

**Quick Meaning.** A state after death the 1517 Theses still reason about, which by 1531 "Scripture does not teach" and by 1542 is among the "popish horrors" abolished from this world's burials.

**World Meaning.** In 1517 the word is still furniture: "Hell, purgatory, and heaven seem to differ as do despair, almost-despair, and the assurance of safety" (v1 1213–1214), and the dispute is only whether the pope's keys reach it. By 1531 the confessional voice says flatly that eternal punishments commuted "to the punishments of purgatory" is a scheme Scripture does not teach (Ap 5005; 6363–6365), and that the keys "have not the power of binding and loosing except upon earth" (Ap 6376–6377). By 1542 the founder's funeral preface counts it among the things "abolished, done away, and out-and-out made an end of the popish horrors, such as wakes, masses for the soul, obsequies, purgatory, and all other mummeries for the dead" (Hy 975–978), against "our doctrine of the resurrection, not that of purgatory with its pains and expiations" (Hy 1015–1017). The word's arc is the world's own: reasoned with, then denied, then buried.

**Ecological Function.** The object 1.1 was said to reach and 1.6 was said to govern; refused in 9.4's burial practice. A [PV]-shaped arc across periods, disclosed rather than tagged (Doc_03 declined [PV] here; agreed — the change is in the world's position, not a plurality of voices).

**Distortion Risk.** *Modern Hearing:* that this world never used the word, or that its founder denied it from the first day. *World Hearing:* a word he reasoned inside in 1517 and the movement had refused by 1531 — a real change the entry states rather than flattens.

**Key Texts.** v1 1213–1214; Ap 6363–6377 (read this pass); Hy 975–978, 1015–1017 (Bacon's English). **Key Sources.** [R2]; [R38]; [R28] the 1542 preface. Author Gravity: none.

**Related Terms.** 1.1 indulgence; 1.6 the keys; 9.4 death.

#### 1.6 the keys / power of the keys — Tier 2 (Doc_03: 1 ↓)

**Tags:** [SC] [TC] [DR] [RT]. **AG:** none. **Registry:** R2, R11, R12, R37, R38. **Living tradition:** 1517–1545 function only.

**Quick Meaning.** Christ's word "Whatsoever thou shalt bind," heard in this world as a promise that calls forth faith and a ministry given to the whole community — not a power, and not the pope's alone.

**World Meaning.** "The keys were not given to Peter alone, but to the whole community… the keys were not ordained for doctrine or government, but only for the binding and loosing of sin" (v2 2388–2392). What happens when they are used is not the exercise of a power: "He calls forth the faith of the penitent… Here there is no mention at all of power, but of the ministry of him that absolves" (v2 8917–8921); to claim them for the pope alone is "a wicked usurpation of power" (v2 8927–8928).
